<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.

If explicit user input is required for another reason before progress can safely continue, do not use the `request_user_input` tool. Ask the user directly with one concise plain-text question instead. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.
</collaboration_mode>

<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

## developer

You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

One authoritative page per\n   subject — update the existing page rather than growing a parallel one.\n6. Update `wiki/index.md`.\n7. Append to `wiki/log.md`: `## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | <title>` with the source\n   path and which pages changed.\n\nOne clip at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch.\n\n## Transcripts\n\nGemini session notes live in `raw/data-about-me/transcripts/` as `.docx` (plus\noccasional `.txt` chat/caption exports). This is unprocessed PII and git-ignored\n— never read the folder wholesale into context.\n\n**Start with the inventory. Do not re-derive the mapping by hand.**\n\n```\npython3 system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py             # full report\npython3 system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --unmatched # only the gaps\npython3 system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --json      # machine-readable\n```\n\nIt parses every filename shape present, maps each transcript to a student folder\nusing `students/_aliases.md`, and prints what it cannot resolve. It is read-only.\n\nThen, per transcript being ingested:\n\n1. Confirm the student mapping from the inventory. If the file is unmapped,\n   resolve it before filing:\n   - a name variant of an existing student → add it to the `## Aliases` section\n     of `students/_aliases.md`, re-run the inventory, continue;\n   - a consultation or one-off, not an ongoing student → add to\n     `## Not students` so it stops surfacing;\n   - a real recurring student with no folder → **ask Joshua before creating\n     one.** Creating a student folder is a judgment call about the\n     relationship, not a filing detail.\n2. Read the transcript. Extract: what was taught, what the student struggled\n   with, what was assigned, what to follow up on.\n3. Write the distillation into the student\'s folder, respecting the\n   primary/mirror boundary in `students/README.md`:\n   - teaching content and judgments → `teaching-notes.md` (**primary** — append,\n     never overwrite, never regenerate)\n   - a dated session record → `transcripts.md` (primary)\n   - anything needing a follow-up action → `open-loops.md`\n   - never write to a `*.generated.md` file; those mirror a live backend.\n4. Append to `wiki/log.md`.\n\nFiling a distillation is not the same as copying the transcript. The `.docx`\nstays in `raw/`; what lands in the student folder is what a human would want to\nread before the next session.\n\n## Bulk exports\n\nTakeout and platform dumps land in `raw/data-about-me/` and are git-ignored PII.\n\n- Never read one wholesale — it will blow the context window and has before.\n- Inventory first (file counts, types, date ranges), report, and let Joshua pick\n  what matters.\n- Duplicate exports are common and large. Report duplicates by hash and size;\n  do not delete without asking.\n- If analysis is needed across many files, delegate to a sub-agent that returns\n  a summary, not file contents.\n\n## Hard rules\n\n- **Never delete or move a source.** Flag duplicates; Joshua decides.\n- **Never create a student folder without asking.**\n- **Never write to `*.generated.md`.** Those are backend mirrors and get\n  overwritten.\n- **Dedupe before ingesting, every time.** The same clip has been ingested twice\n  before because the check was skipped.\n- **One source at a time, supervised,** unless Joshua explicitly says batch.\n- **Government IDs stay out.** If an export contains them, stop and say so.\n- Prefer the inventory script over hand-parsing filenames. If it gets something\n  wrong, fix the script or the alias file — do not work around it silently, or\n  the next session re-derives the same mapping.\n\r\n'}]

After installation, start the agent inside Herdr:\n\n# Agent skill file\n\nHerdr ships a reusable agent skill file at `skills/herdr/SKILL.md`.\n\nInstall that file into any coding agent that supports reusable skills or custom instructions. The skill teaches the agent how to control Herdr from inside a Herdr pane.\n\nHerdr also serves a separate guide at `herdr.dev/agent-guide.md` for a different job: an agent helping a human learn, set up, or troubleshoot Herdr. The skill is for an agent operating Herdr; the guide is for an agent teaching a human.\n\n## What the skill does\n\nSection titled “What the skill does”\n\nThe skill tells an agent to use the `herdr` CLI when `HERDR_ENV=1` is set. That means the agent is running inside a Herdr-managed pane and can safely talk to the local Herdr socket.\n\nWith the skill installed, an agent can:\n\n  * inspect workspaces, tabs, panes, and neighboring agents\n  * split panes and run commands without stealing focus\n  * read pane output and recent logs\n  * wait for servers, tests, or another agent to finish\n  * start helper agents in sibling panes\n\nThe skill is a Markdown instruction file for agents. If Herdr is already installed, run `herdr --skill` to print the release-matched copy bundled with that binary.\n\n## Install it\n\nSection titled “Install it”\n\nInstall the skill with `npx skills`:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    npx skills add herdrdev/herdr --skill herdr -g\n    \n    `\n\nIf you installed the skill while it lived at the repository root, rerun this add command once instead of using `skills update`. The add command replaces the oversized copy and records the new location.\n\nThe `-g` flag installs it globally for supported agents. Omit `-g` to install it into the current project.\n\nUse the repository copy as the manual fallback and source of truth:\n    \n    `\n    \n    https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/v0.9.1/skills/herdr/SKILL.md\n    \n    `\n\nFor agents with a skill system, install that file as a skill named `herdr`. For agents without a skill system, paste the file into the agent’s project or user instructions.\n\nAfter installation, start the agent inside Herdr:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    herdr\n    \n    claude\n    \n    `\n\nOr use any other coding agent in a Herdr pane so `HERDR_ENV=1` is available to the agent process.\n\n## Safety rule\n\nSection titled “Safety rule”\n\nThe skill starts with one guardrail: if `HERDR_ENV=1` is not set, the agent should stop and say it is not running inside a Herdr-managed pane.\n\nThis prevents an agent outside Herdr from trying to control a session it does not own.\n\n## Agent-facing reference\n\nSection titled “Agent-facing reference”\n\nThe full command guide lives in the skill file itself. It covers pane IDs, `pane split`, `pane run`, `pane read`, `pane wait-output`, `agent wait`, workspace and tab commands, and coordination recipes.\n\nRead the source file here:\n\nOpen `SKILL.md` on GitHub →--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nherdr/AGENTS.md at master · ogulcancelik/herdr · GitHub (https://github.com/ogulcancelik/herdr/blob/master/AGENTS.md)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search1\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last month; This section applies only on Can\'s workstation or Windows VM setup when the acting GitHub account is `ogulcancelik`. ... Treat the acting account as a verified maintainer only when its username is listed there, the configured remote is the canonical `ogulcancelik/herdr` repository, and the authenticated account has write access to that repository. If any condition cannot be verified, skip maintainer workflow and follow the external contributor guardrail instead.\n  * Local Can machine workflow applies only on Can\'s own workstation or Windows VM setup, for example when `/home/can/Projects/herdr`, `HERDR_ENV=1`, or the `windows-wirt` SSH alias exists. If those facts are not true, skip local machine workflow.\n  * External contributor guardrail applies whenever the acting GitHub account is not a verified maintainer, the work is happening in a fork, or the account cannot be determined.\n\n## Universal Project Rules\n\n### Principles\n\n  * State is separated from runtime. `AppState` is pure data, testable without PTYs or async. `PaneState` is separate from `PaneRuntime`. Workspace logic doesn\'t need real terminals.\n  * Render is pure. `compute_view()` handles geometry and mutations. `render()` takes `&AppState` and only draws. Never mutate state during render.\n  * No god objects. If a module is doing too many things, split it. `app/` is already split into state, actions, and input. ## Local Can Machine Workflow\n\nThis section applies only on Can\'s workstation or Windows VM setup when the acting GitHub account is `ogulcancelik`. Other verified maintainers skip this local-machine section but continue following maintainer workflow. Everyone else follows the external contributor guardrail.\n\n### Windows VM validation\n\nThe Windows VM is for final/manual Windows validation, not normal agent work. Connect to it with the `windows-wirt` SSH alias.\n\nUse the single reusable checkout at `C:\\work\\repo`. Do not create additional persistent Herdr clones or worktrees on the VM. The Windows account is already named `herdr`, so avoid paths like `C:\\Users\\herdr\\herdr`.\n\nBefore validating a fix on Windows, sync or apply the Linux worktree changes into `C:\\work\\repo`, then run the needed Windows build or test commands there. Reuse the shared Rust caches under `C:\\Users\\herdr\\.cargo` and `C:\\Users\\herdr\\.rustup`. Do not use WSL on the VM. The VM may have a newer Zig on `PATH`; Herdr currently requires Zig 0.15.2, so set `$env:ZIG = "C:\\Users\\herdr\\zig-0.15.2\\zig.exe"` before running Cargo commands that build the vendored libghostty-vt.\n\nAfter validation, leave `C:\\work\\repo` clean. Remove temporary files and delete `C:\\work\\repo\\target` when disk space is tight, but keep the shared Cargo and Rustup caches. Unless Can explicitly asks to keep the patched tree for more manual testing, reset `C:\\work\\repo` back to a clean checkout before finishing.\n\n## Agent Detection Updates\n\nAgent detection changes should use the manifest hot-reload loop. Use the project-local `herdr-throwaway-repro` skill to create a disposable named session and drive the real agent UI through Herdr\'s CLI/API into the target state. Read the pane with `herdr agent read <pane> --source detection --format text` and inspect matching with `herdr agent explain <pane> --json`. Update the bundled manifest in `src/detect/manifests/<agent>.toml`, copy that manifest to the local override path at `~/.config/herdr/agent-detection/<agent>.toml`, then run `herdr server reload-agent-manifests` against the session under test. Before writing the override, check whether one already exists; never overwrite or remove a pre-existing override without alignment. Once the rule is correct, remove the temporary override or restore the previous one exactly so the committed bundled manifest remains the source of truth.\n\nDo not add large agent-specific full-screen fixture suites for routine manifest tuning. Keep Rust tests focused on manifest parsing, rule semantics, skip-state semantics, source precedence, cache reload behavior, and update flow. Use live pane reads for agent-specific screen evidence.\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nIssues · ogulcancelik/herdr · GitHub The agent-oriented workflow is documented in `skills/herdr-browser/SKILL.md`.\n## Install\n\nInstall the published plugin from GitHub:\n    \n    herdr plugin install ogulcancelik/herdr-browser --yes\n\nFor local development, link this checkout instead:\n    \n    herdr plugin link ~/Projects/herdr-browser\n\n## Agent Browser Automation\n\nHerdr Browser includes a CLI in the plugin directory; it does not install a global executable. Find the active checkout with:\n    \n    herdr plugin list --plugin official.browser --json\n\nRead `result.plugins[0].plugin_root`, then run the CLI with Bun:\n    \n    bun run "<plugin_root>/src/cli.ts" views\n    bun run "<plugin_root>/src/cli.ts" connect --view <view_id>\n\n`views` lists browser views currently backed by visible plugin panes. `connect` starts a loopback CDP gateway scoped to the selected view and returns its HTTP and WebSocket endpoints. The gateway represents the complete browser view, not only its active tab. Browser Use, PinchTab, Playwright, and other CDP clients can navigate and manage tabs through their normal APIs while the Herdr tab strip and rendered page follow standard CDP target activation.\n\nPoint a client at the returned endpoint:\n\n  * Browser Use: set `BU_CDP_URL` to `cdp_http_url` or `BU_CDP_WS` to `browser_ws_url`.\n  * Playwright: call `chromium.connectOverCDP(cdp_http_url)`.\n  * Playwright MCP: pass `--cdp-endpoint=<cdp_http_url>`.\n  * Chrome DevTools MCP: pass `--browser-url=<cdp_http_url>`.\n\nWhile a client is connected, the pane stays fully interactive. Clicks, scrolling, hover, typing, and the toolbar act on the same targets the automation client sees, so you can intervene mid-run and hand control back without reconnecting.\n\nThe endpoint grants full control over that browser view. Keep it local and do not expose it over the network. Herdr Browser retains ownership of Chromium; closing an attached automation client disconnects it without terminating the plugin browser.\n\nThe agent-oriented workflow is documented in `skills/herdr-browser/SKILL.md`.\n\n## Open A Browser Pane\n\nOpen a blank browser in a right split:\n    \n    herdr plugin pane open \\\n      --plugin official.browser \\\n      --entrypoint browser \\\n      --placement split \\\n      --direction right \\\n      --focus\n\nUse `--placement tab`, `--placement zoomed`, or `--placement overlay` for the other supported layouts. The first toolbar row provides tab selection, close, and new-tab controls. The second provides back, forward, reload, stop, zoom, and URL entry. Page clicks, scrolling, hover, and keyboard input are forwarded to Chromium.\n\nPass an initial URL when another plugin opens the pane:\n    \n    herdr plugin pane open \\\n      --plugin official.browser \\\n      --entrypoint browser \\\n      --placement zoomed \\\n      --env HERDR_BROWSER_INITIAL_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3000 \\\n      --focus\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nherdr-browser/skills/herdr-browser/SKILL.md at main · ogulcancelik/herdr-browser · GitHub (https://github.com/ogulcancelik/herdr-browser/blob/main/skills/herdr-browser/SKILL.md)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search4\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last month; Do not install `herdr-browser` globally. ...     herdr plugin list --plugin official.browser --json\n\nEdit and raw actions\n\nname  | herdr-browser\ndescription  | Connect browser automation tools to a Chromium view rendered inside a Herdr browser pane.\n\n# Herdr Browser\n\nUse this skill when the user wants Browser Use, PinchTab, Playwright, Chrome DevTools MCP, or another CDP client to control the browser visible in Herdr.\n\nDo not install `herdr-browser` globally. Run the CLI with Bun from any working directory:\n    \n    bun run "<plugin_root>/src/cli.ts"\n\nList live browser views before connecting:\n    \n    bun run "<plugin_root>/src/cli.ts" views\n\nSelect the intended view from its `view_id`, `pane_id`, URL, title, and tabs. Never guess when more than one view is present. Windows is generally available, with documented platform-specific limitations and ongoing fixes.\n\n## Install\n\nSection titled “Install”\n\nOn Linux or macOS, run:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    curl -fsSL https://herdr.dev/install.sh | sh\n    \n    `\n\nOn Windows, run:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -c "irm https://herdr.dev/install.ps1 | iex"\n    \n    `\n\nIf endpoint security blocks that fileless PowerShell command, open Command Prompt and run:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    curl.exe -fsSLo install.cmd https://herdr.dev/install.cmd && install.cmd && del install.cmd\n    \n    `\n\nThe installer downloads the release binary for your platform and places it on your PATH. New direct installs use the stable update channel. Existing Windows preview installs stay on preview until you switch them with `herdr channel set stable`. On Windows, PATH points to the active versioned release directory; the installer also updates the `current` and stable `bin` compatibility aliases, so updates do not need to overwrite a running `herdr.exe`.\n\nThe flake also exposes a development shell:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    nix develop github:herdrdev/herdr\n    \n    `\n\nUse the same Nix workflow to update Herdr. For a profile install, list your profile entries and upgrade the Herdr entry:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    nix profile list\n    \n    nix profile upgrade <index-or-name>\n    \n    `\n\nIf Herdr is an input in your own flake, update that input and rebuild your system, Home Manager, or development environment:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    nix flake update herdr\n    \n    `\n\n## Download manually\n\nSection titled “Download manually”\n\nYou can also download a binary from GitHub releases.\n\nChoose the asset that matches your system:\n\nSystem  | Asset\n--- | ---\nLinux x86_64  | `herdr-linux-x86_64`\nLinux aarch64  | `herdr-linux-aarch64`\nmacOS Intel  | `herdr-macos-x86_64`\nmacOS Apple silicon  | `herdr-macos-aarch64`\nWindows x86_64  | `herdr-windows-x86_64.zip`\n\nOn Linux or macOS, make it executable and move it somewhere on your PATH.\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    chmod +x herdr-linux-x86_64\n    \n    mv herdr-linux-x86_64 ~/.local/bin/herdr\n    \n    `\n\n### Windows archive\n\nSection titled “Windows archive”\n\nStable releases and preview prereleases both include `herdr-windows-x86_64.zip`. The archive contains `herdr.exe` and its app-local ConPTY runtime. Keep the extracted directory together; do not copy only `herdr.exe`.\n\n## Verify\n\nSection titled “Verify”\n\nStart Herdr:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    herdr\n    \n    `\n\nIf your shell cannot find `herdr`, restart the terminal or check that the install directory is on your PATH.\n\n## Update\n\nSection titled “Update”\n\nHerdr checks for new releases and notifies you in the app. You can update manually:\n\nTerminal window\n    \n    `\n    \n    herdr update\n    \n    `\n\nUse `herdr update` only for installs managed by Herdr’s own installer. Most core workflows are supported, but some capabilities differ from Linux and macOS or remain platform-dependent. New installs use stable by default, and stable is recommended for normal use. Existing preview installs stay on preview until you run `herdr channel set stable`. Remote clipboard images and image-file drops  | supported\n`cmd.exe` panes  | supported\nNative keyboard and mouse input  | supported\nStartup cwd and workspace labels  | supported\nPane launch cwd  | supported\nAgent command discovery  | supported\nSupported agent self-report integrations  | supported\nAgent process-tree detection  | supported\nGit/worktree detection from known cwd  | supported\nSystem notifications and MP3 sounds  | supported\nPlugins  | preview\nPane screen history  | supported\nNested launch override  | supported\n\nLocal persistent sessions continue running after the client detaches or its terminal window closes. Servers and pane processes launched through Windows OpenSSH also survive logout; run `herdr` again to reconnect.\n\nWindows agent process detection scans descendants of the pane shell and recognizes direct agents plus common command wrappers, including npm/Node and Git Bash process chains. Availability is narrower than on Unix; Herdr hides or rejects integrations whose install format is not supported on Windows.\n\nPlugins support `windows` as a manifest platform in preview. Commands are argv commands and must be Windows-compatible; Node package shims such as `npm`, `bun`, and `node` are expected to work when they are on `PATH`, while Unix-only examples using `sh` or Bash need Windows-specific alternatives. Platform filters skip unsupported build commands and return `platform_unsupported` for unsupported actions or panes.\n\n    `\n\nThe target host can run Linux, macOS, or Windows. On Windows hosts, remote attach reuses a compatible package from `PATH` or the active managed release. Interactive direct attach and saved-machine setup prompt before installing or updating the complete package when needed. Background saved reconnect only discovers installed packages and cannot prompt for installation or updates. Herdr uses the installed Windows OpenSSH client and your SSH configuration. Windows OpenSSH does not use Herdr’s Unix control-socket reuse, so key authentication through Windows `ssh-agent` is recommended to avoid repeated prompts during remote setup.\n\nWindows updates run through the Windows installer and update the active versioned release path. New terminals and reconnected SSH sessions receive that path; start Herdr there to use the updated client. Compatible running servers keep their panes alive. Restart a server later only when you need server-side changes from the release. The prefix is `ctrl+b`, panes persist, detach and reattach work the way you expect.\n\nConcepts → · Keybindings →\n\n## Or let your agent introduce you\n\nSection titled “Or let your agent introduce you”\n\nIf you already run an AI coding agent, let it handle the onboarding. Paste this prompt:\n    \n    `\n    \n    Help me understand and set up Herdr. Read https://herdr.dev/agent-guide.md first, then walk me through it step by step.\n    \n    `\n\nThe guide covers Herdr’s concepts, setup, configuration, and common fixes, so your agent can answer from the docs instead of improvising.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nherdr/distribution/agent-guide.md at master · herdrdev/herdr · GitHub (https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/master/distribution/agent-guide.md)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search10\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: yesterday; Herdr ships `skills/herdr/SKILL.md` (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/herdrdev/herdr/master/skills/herdr/SKILL.md), which teaches a coding agent to control Herdr from inside a pane: splitting panes, running commands without stealing focus, reading output, and waiting on other agents. ... For agents without a skill system, add the GitHub copy above to their global custom instructions. ...   * Config file: `~/.config/herdr/config.toml` on Linux and macOS; `%APPDATA%\\herdr\\config.toml` on Windows.\n## The keyboard story\n\nNew users do not need to learn keybindings; the mouse covers everything. When the human wants keyboard control:\n\n  * The prefix key is `ctrl+b` by default. `prefix+?` shows every active binding live.\n  * The guided keyboard page covers the prefix, the bindings to learn first, and a vetted prefix-free setup using `ctrl+alt` chords: https://herdr.dev/docs/keyboard/. Recommend it over improvising.\n  * Every binding, including the prefix itself, is configurable under `[keys]` in the config file.\n  * If a direct chord does nothing, the OS or the outer terminal consumed it before Herdr could see it. The keyboard page explains which chords are safe and why.\n\n## Install the Herdr skill into yourself\n\nHerdr ships `skills/herdr/SKILL.md` (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/herdrdev/herdr/master/skills/herdr/SKILL.md), which teaches a coding agent to control Herdr from inside a pane: splitting panes, running commands without stealing focus, reading output, and waiting on other agents.\n\nOnce the human is set up, offer to install it for your coding agent so future sessions can control Herdr directly. For agents supported by the open skills CLI, use `npx skills add herdrdev/herdr --skill herdr -g`. For agents without a skill system, add the GitHub copy above to their global custom instructions. Ask the human before writing to their config locations, and use the GitHub copy above as the source of truth.\n\n## Configuration\n\n  * Config file: `~/.config/herdr/config.toml` on Linux and macOS; `%APPDATA%\\herdr\\config.toml` on Windows. Herdr works without one.\n  * Print the full default config: `herdr --default-config`.\n  * Apply edits to a running server: `herdr server reload-config` (or the global menu → reload config).\n  * Main areas: `[keys]` keybindings, `[theme]` themes, `[ui]` sidebar and UI behavior, `[terminal]` shell defaults, `[update]` channel.\n  * Full reference: https://herdr.dev/docs/configuration/\n\n## Diagnosis recipes\n\n  * Agent not detected or wrong state: Run `herdr agent list` to see what Herdr sees and `herdr agent explain <target> --json` to see why the detector classified a pane that way. --------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nAgents | herdr (https://herdr.dev/docs/agents/)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search11\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; After editing a local override manually, restart Herdr or run `herdr server reload-agent-manifests` to apply the file to the running server. ... Install the integration for each agent you use to give Herdr hook or plugin reports instead of screen detection alone:\n\n# Agents\n\nHerdr is built for running more than one coding agent at a time. Each agent stays in a real terminal pane with its shell, logs, prompts, and running processes intact. Herdr tracks which panes contain agents, rolls their state up to tabs and workspaces, and lets you jump straight to the pane that needs attention instead of polling every terminal by hand.\n\nTo coordinate agents from scripts or from another agent, see Agent automation.\n\n## Supported agents\n\nSection titled “Supported agents”\n\nAutomatic detection works out of the box for common coding agents. The table shows which signal determines `idle`, `working`, and `blocked` for each one.\n\nAgent  | State authority  | Integration role\n--- | --- | ---\nPi  | lifecycle hooks when installed; otherwise screen manifest  | state and session\nOMP  | lifecycle hooks when installed  | state and session\nGitHub Copilot CLI  | screen manifest  | session\nDevin CLI  | screen manifest  | session\nKimi Code CLI  | lifecycle hooks when installed; otherwise screen manifest  | state and session\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nAD Bridge\nQuick Start Guide\nTC:1/8/2020\n©2003-2019 (https://github.com/BeyondTrust/pbis-open/wiki/docs/adb-quick-start-guide.pdf)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn0search12\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 1.3 years ago; Install the Agent, Join a Domain, and Log On ... \ue200cite\ue20227†Install with Homebrew\ue201 L55:   * \ue200cite\ue20228†Install with mise\ue201 L56:   * \ue200cite\ue20229†Install with Nix\ue201 L57:   * \ue200cite\ue20230†Download \nL63: # Install Herdr\nL64: \nL65: Herdr publishes stable-channel binaries for Linux, macOS, and Windows. Windows is generally available, with documented platform-specific limitations and ongoing fixes.\nL66: ## Install\nL67: \nL68: \ue200cite\ue20226†Section titled “Install”\ue201 L69: \nL70: On Linux or macOS, run:\nL71: \nL72: Terminal window\nL73: \nL74:     `\nL75: \nL76:     curl -fsSL https://herdr.dev/install.sh | sh\nL77: \nL78:     `\nL79: \nL80: On Windows, run:\nL81: \nL82: Terminal window\nL83: \nL84:     `\nL85: \nL86:     powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -c "irm https://herdr.dev/install.ps1 | iex"\nL87: \nL88:     `\nL89: \nL90: If endpoint security blocks that fileless PowerShell command, open Command Prompt and run:\nL91: Terminal window\nL92: \nL93:     `\nL94: \nL95:     curl.exe -fsSLo install.cmd https://herdr.dev/install.cmd && install.cmd && del install.cmd\nL96: \nL97:     `\nL98: The installer downloads the release binary for your platform and places it on your PATH. New direct installs use the stable update channel. Existing Windows preview installs stay on preview until you switch them with `herdr channel set stable`. On Windows, PATH points to the active versioned release directory; the installer also updates the `current` and stable `bin` compatibility aliases, so updates do not need to overwrite a running `herdr.exe`.\nL99: ## Install with Homebrew\nL100: \nL101: \ue200cite\ue20227†Section titled “Install with Homebrew”\ue201 L102: \nL103: If you already use Homebrew:\nL104: \nL105: Terminal window\nL106: \nL107:     `\nL108: \nL109:     brew install herdr\nL110: \nL111:     `\nL112: ## Install with mise\nL113: \nL114: \ue200cite\ue20228†Section titled “Install with mise”\ue201 L115: \nL116: If you already use mise:\nL117: \nL118: Terminal window\nL119: \nL120:     `\nL121: \nL122:     mise use -g herdr\nL123: \nL124:     `\nL125: \nL126: If mise reports `herdr not found in mise tool registry`, update mise and retry. Older mise versions predate the Herdr registry entry; `mise use -g github:herdrdev/herdr` works as a temporary fallback.\nL127: ## Install with Nix\nL128: \nL129: \ue200cite\ue20229†Section titled “Install with Nix”\ue201 L130: \nL131: If you already use Nix, Herdr provides a flake that builds Herdr from source:\nL132: \nL133: Terminal window\nL134: \nL135:     `\nL136: \nL137:     nix run github:herdrdev/herdr/v0.x.y\nL138: \nL139:     nix build github:herdrdev/herdr/v0.x.y\nL140: \nL141:     nix profile install github:herdrdev/herdr/v0.x.y\nL142: \nL143:     `\nL144: \nL145: Replace `v0.x.y` with the latest release tag. You can omit the tag to track `master`, but release tags are recommended for normal installs.\nL146: The flake also exposes a development shell:\nL147: \nL148: Terminal window\nL149: \nL150:     `\nL151: \nL152:     nix develop github:herdrdev/herdr\nL153: \nL154:     `\nL155: \nL156: Use the same Nix workflow to update Herdr. For a profile install, list your profile entries and upgrade the Herdr entry:\nL157: \nL158: Terminal window\nL159: \nL160:     `\nL161: \nL162:     nix profile list\nL163: \nL164:     nix profile upgrade <index-or-name>\nL165: \nL166:     `\nL167: \nL168: If Herdr is an input in your own flake, update that input and rebuild your system, Home Manager, or development environment:\nL169: Terminal window\nL170: \nL171:     `\nL172: \nL173:     nix flake update herdr\nL174: \nL175:     `\nL176: ## Download manually\nL177: \nL178: \ue200cite\ue20230†Section titled “Download manually”\ue201 L179: \nL180: You can also download a binary from \ue200cite\ue20235†GitHub releases†github.com\ue201 .\nL181: \nL182: Choose the asset that matches your system:\nL183: \nL184: System  | Asset\nL185: --- | ---\nL186: Linux x86_64  | `herdr-linux-x86_64`\nL187: Linux aarch64  | `herdr-linux-aarch64`\nL188: macOS Intel  | `herdr-macos-x86_64`\nL189: macOS Apple silicon  | `herdr-macos-aarch64`\nL190: Windows x86_64  | `herdr-windows-x86_64.zip`\nL191: \nL192: On Linux or macOS, make it executable and move it somewhere on your PATH.\nL193: Terminal window\nL194: \nL195:     `\nL196: \nL197:     chmod +x herdr-linux-x86_64\nL198: \nL199:     mv herdr-linux-x86_64 ~/.local/bin/herdr\nL200: \nL201:     `\nL202: ### Windows archive\nL203: \nL204: \ue200cite\ue20231†Section titled “Windows archive”\ue201 L205: \nL206: Stable releases and preview prereleases both include `herdr-windows-x86_64.zip`. The archive contains `herdr.exe` and its app-local ConPTY runtime. Keep the extracted directory together; do not copy only `herdr.exe`.\nL207: \nL208: ## Verify\nL209: \nL210: \ue200cite\ue20232†Section titled “Verify”\ue201 L211: \nL212: Start Herdr:\nL213: \nL214: Terminal window\nL215: \nL216:     `\nL217: \nL218:     herdr\nL219: \nL220:     `\nL221: \nL222: If your shell cannot find `herdr`, restart the terminal or check that the install directory is on your PATH.\nL223: ## Update\nL224: \nL225: \ue200cite\ue20233†Section titled “Update”\ue201 L226: \nL227: Herdr checks for new releases and notifies you in the app. You can update manually:\nL228: \nL229: Terminal window\nL230: \nL231:     `\nL232: \nL233:     herdr update\nL234: \nL235:     `\nL236: \nL237: Use `herdr update` only for installs managed by Herdr’s own installer. Update Homebrew, mise, and Nix installs through those package managers instead.\nL238: \nL239: Direct installs on Linux, macOS, and Windows use the stable update channel by default. To opt into preview builds from `master`, set the channel:\nL240: Terminal window\nL241: \nL242:     `\nL243: \nL244:     herdr channel set preview\nL245: \nL246:     `\nL247: \nL248: Switch a direct install back to stable the same way:\nL249: \nL250: Terminal window\nL251: \nL252:     `\nL253: \nL254:     herdr channel set stable\nL255: \nL256:     `\nL257: \nL258: For direct installs, changing channels checks the selected channel and installs its latest binary. If that update fails, run `herdr update` to retry from the configured channel.\nL259: Preview builds are regularly published GitHub prereleases from the current development branch. They are useful when you want fixes before the next stable release, but they can regress. Homebrew, mise, and Nix installs do not use the preview channel.\nL260: Stable is the recommended channel for normal Windows use. Preview receives fixes sooner but can regress, so opt in only when you want that tradeoff. Existing Windows preview installs remain on preview until you switch them explicitly. If an older preview build rejects `herdr channel set stable`, run `herdr update` once on preview, then retry the channel switch.\nL261: By default, `herdr update` installs the new binary and leaves endpoint-generation-1 servers and their pane processes running. Start Herdr again to reconnect with the updated client. Client-only changes work immediately. A new server-side action stays unavailable until that server is updated and restarted; the client shows a notice instead of disconnecting. Servers from before endpoint generation 1 need one final stop during update.\nL262: To opt into experimental live server handoff for supported running sessions, run:\nL263: \nL264: Terminal window\nL265: \nL266:     `\nL267: \nL268:     herdr update --handoff\nL269: \nL270:     `\nL271: Live handoff does not apply to Homebrew, mise, or Nix package-manager updates. For those installs, update with the package manager, then run Herdr again to reconnect with the updated client. The compatible old server keeps running. Restart it later with `herdr server stop` or `herdr session stop <name>` only when you want server-side changes from the new release.\nL272: ## Requirements\nL273: \nL274: \ue200cite\ue20234†Section titled “Requirements”\ue201 L275: \nL276: Stable-channel binaries are available for Linux, macOS, and Windows x86_64. See \ue200cite\ue20223†Windows support\ue201 for supported workflows and known limitations. Windows ARM64 runs the x86_64 build under Windows emulation.\nL277: \nL278: \ue200cite\ue20236†Edit page†github.com\ue201 L279: \nL280: \ue200cite\ue2022†Previous Overview\ue201 L281: \ue200cite\ue2026†Next Quick start\ue201 --------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nAgent skill file | herdr
