\ue200cite\ue20229†Agent-facing reference\ue201 L45: ## On this page\nL46: \nL47:   * \ue200cite\ue2020†Overview\ue201 L48:   * \ue200cite\ue20226†What the skill does\ue201 L49:   * \ue200cite\ue20227†Install it\ue201 L50:   * \ue200cite\ue20228†Safety rule\ue201 L51:   * \ue200cite\ue20229†Agent-facing reference\ue201 L52: # Agent skill file\nL53: \nL54: Herdr ships a reusable agent skill file at \ue200cite\ue20230†`skills/herdr/SKILL.md`†github.com\ue201 .\nL55: \nL56: Install that file into any coding agent that supports reusable skills or custom instructions. The skill teaches the agent how to control Herdr from inside a Herdr pane.\nL57: Herdr also serves a separate guide at \ue200cite\ue20231†`herdr.dev/agent-guide.md`\ue201 for a different job: an agent helping a human learn, set up, or troubleshoot Herdr. The skill is for an agent operating Herdr; the guide is for an agent teaching a human.\nL58: ## What the skill does\nL59: \nL60: \ue200cite\ue20226†Section titled “What the skill does”\ue201 L61: \nL62: The skill tells an agent to use the `herdr` CLI when `HERDR_ENV=1` is set. That means the agent is running inside a Herdr-managed pane and can safely talk to the local Herdr socket.\nL63: \nL64: With the skill installed, an agent can:\nL65:   * inspect workspaces, tabs, panes, and neighboring agents\nL66:   * split panes and run commands without stealing focus\nL67:   * read pane output and recent logs\nL68:   * wait for servers, tests, or another agent to finish\nL69:   * start helper agents in sibling panes\nL70: \nL71: The skill is a Markdown instruction file for agents. If Herdr is already installed, run `herdr --skill` to print the release-matched copy bundled with that binary.\nL72: ## Install it\nL73: \nL74: \ue200cite\ue20227†Section titled “Install it”\ue201 L75: \nL76: Install the skill with `npx skills`:\nL77: \nL78: Terminal window\nL79: \nL80:     `\nL81: \nL82:     npx skills add herdrdev/herdr --skill herdr -g\nL83: \nL84:     `\nL85: \nL86: If you installed the skill while it lived at the repository root, rerun this add command once instead of using `skills update`. The add command replaces the oversized copy and records the new location.\nL87: \nL88: The `-g` flag installs it globally for supported agents. Omit `-g` to install it into the current project.\nL89: Use the repository copy as the manual fallback and source of truth:\nL90: \nL91:     `\nL92: \nL93:     https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/v0.9.1/skills/herdr/SKILL.md\nL94: \nL95:     `\nL96: \nL97: For agents with a skill system, install that file as a skill named `herdr`. For agents without a skill system, paste the file into the agent’s project or user instructions.\nL98: \nL99: After installation, start the agent inside Herdr:\nL100: \nL101: Terminal window\nL102: \nL103:     `\nL104: \nL105:     herdr\nL106: \nL107:     claude\nL108: \nL109:     `\nL110: Or use any other coding agent in a Herdr pane so `HERDR_ENV=1` is available to the agent process.\nL111: ## Safety rule\nL112: \nL113: \ue200cite\ue20228†Section titled “Safety rule”\ue201 L114: \nL115: The skill starts with one guardrail: if `HERDR_ENV=1` is not set, the agent should stop and say it is not running inside a Herdr-managed pane.\nL116: \nL117: This prevents an agent outside Herdr from trying to control a session it does not own.\nL118: ## Agent-facing reference\nL119: \nL120: \ue200cite\ue20229†Section titled “Agent-facing reference”\ue201 L121: \nL122: The full command guide lives in the skill file itself. It covers pane IDs, `pane split`, `pane run`, `pane read`, `pane wait-output`, `agent wait`, workspace and tab commands, and coordination recipes.\nL123: \nL124: Read the source file here:\nL125: \nL126: \ue200cite\ue20230†Open `SKILL.md` on GitHub →†github.com\ue201 L127: \nL128: \ue200cite\ue20232†Edit page†github.com\ue201 L129: \nL130: \ue200cite\ue20221†Previous Integrations\ue201 L131: \ue200cite\ue20223†Next Windows support\ue201 --------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nAgents | herdr (https://herdr.dev/docs/agents/)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn1view2\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: open({"ref_id":"turn0search11","lineno":null}); Total lines: 255\nL115: ## Blocked state\nL116: \nL117: \ue200cite\ue20229†Section titled “Blocked state”\ue201 L118: \nL119: Blocked detection is deliberately strict for screen-manifest agents. Herdr only marks `blocked` when the live bottom-buffer snapshot matches known visible approval, question, or permission UI. If no manifest rule matches for a known agent, Herdr falls back to `idle` and labels that fallback as `default_known_agent_idle_fallback` in explain output.\nL120: This means unusual new agent prompts may initially show as `idle` instead of `blocked` until Herdr learns that screen shape. The misclassification affects only the visible status and waits. It should not make Herdr send input or take destructive action.\nL121: ## Detection manifests\nL122: \nL123: \ue200cite\ue20230†Section titled “Detection manifests”\ue201 L124: \nL125: Bundled manifests live inside Herdr. Herdr also checks herdr.dev for remote manifest updates and applies valid per-agent rule updates automatically without requiring a Herdr restart. Remote manifests are stored in Herdr’s state directory. Set `[update] manifest_check = false` to disable background remote manifest checks.\nL126: Local overrides can replace a remote or bundled manifest from the platform config directory:\nL127: \nL128:     `\nL129: \nL130:     ~/.config/herdr/agent-detection/<agent>.toml\nL131: \nL132:     `\nL133: Local overrides always win. Without a local override, Herdr uses the newer compatible manifest between the cached remote manifest and the bundled manifest in the running binary. On debug builds, the same config helper may use a development directory such as `herdr-dev`. Invalid override files are ignored with a warning and Herdr falls back to the cached remote or bundled manifest for that agent.\nL134: Remote manifests patch detection rules for agents Herdr already knows how to identify. Adding a completely new agent still requires a Herdr binary update for process detection, labels, and integration behavior.\nL135: The running server loads active manifests into memory on startup. Automatic remote manifest updates reload that in-memory cache after new rules are written. Run `herdr server update-agent-manifests` to fetch remote manifest updates immediately and reload the running server. After editing a local override manually, restart Herdr or run `herdr server reload-agent-manifests` to apply the file to the running server.\nL136: \nL137: Use `herdr agent explain` when a pane shows the wrong state:\nL138: Terminal window\nL139: \nL140:     `\nL141: \nL142:     herdr agent explain <target>\nL143: \nL144:     herdr agent explain --file screen.txt --agent codex --json\nL145: \nL146:     `\nL147: Live explain is evaluated by the running server, so it reflects the active manifest cache. The explain output shows the agent, final state, whether screen detection was skipped by a full lifecycle authority, manifest source and version, cached remote version, local override shadowing, remote update status, matched rule, visible evidence flags, matcher and region evidence for evaluated rules, skipped-update reason for transcript viewers, and the idle fallback reason when no rule matched.\nL148: Herdr can run inside tmux as the outer terminal environment. Agent detection does not inspect tmux sessions launched inside a Herdr pane. If a shell framework auto-enters tmux inside Herdr, Herdr sees `tmux` as the pane process instead of the agent behind it.\nL149: ## State rollups\nL150: \nL151: \ue200cite\ue20231†Section titled “State rollups”\ue201 L152: \nL153: The sidebar rolls state upward.\nL154: \nL155: A blocked agent makes its pane, tab, and workspace look blocked. A working agent makes the workspace look active. A done agent stays visible until you view it.\nL156: \nL157: This is the main Herdr workflow: start several agents, let them work in parallel, and use the sidebar to see which project needs a decision, which one is still running, and which one is ready to review.\nL158: ## Direct integrations\nL159: \nL160: \ue200cite\ue20232†Section titled “Direct integrations”\ue201 L161: \nL162: Install the integration for each agent you use to give Herdr hook or plugin reports instead of screen detection alone:\nL163: \nL164: Terminal window\nL165: \nL166:     `\nL167: \nL168:     herdr integration install claude\nL169: \nL170:     herdr integration status\nL171: \nL172:     `\nL173: Each supported agent has its own integration name and behavior. See \ue200cite\ue20221†Integrations\ue201 for the per-agent details and the full install list. If you are building an agent, the \ue200cite\ue20236†custom integration guide\ue201 shows how to report lifecycle state without adding native support to Herdr.\nL174: ## Custom agent labels\nL175: \nL176: \ue200cite\ue20233†Section titled “Custom agent labels”\ue201 L177: \nL178: You can rename an agent target for display:\nL179: \nL180: Terminal window\nL181: \nL182:     `\nL183: \nL184:     herdr agent rename w1:p1 reviewer\nL185: \nL186:     herdr agent rename reviewer --clear\nL187: \nL188:     `\nL189: \nL190: Targets accept a unique live agent name or the pane ID that currently hosts the agent. Terminal IDs and bare agent-kind labels are not accepted.\nL191: ## Custom status labels\nL192: \nL193: \ue200cite\ue20234†Section titled “Custom status labels”\ue201 L194: \nL195: Integrations report lifecycle state as semantic state only. Add display customization separately with pane metadata tokens.\nL196: \nL197: Terminal window\nL198: \nL199:     `\nL200: \nL201:     herdr pane report-agent w1:p1 \\\nL202: \nL203:       --source custom:indexer \\\nL204: \nL205:       --agent docs-bot \\\nL206: \nL207:       --state working\nL208: \nL209: \nL210: \nL211: \nL212:     herdr pane report-metadata w1:p1 \\\nL213: \nL214:       --source custom:indexer-display \\\nL215: \nL216:       --token summary=indexing\nL217: \nL218:     `\nL219: `state` controls waits, notifications, and rollups. The `summary` token is display-only and can be used as `$summary` in an Agent sidebar row.\nL220: Agent sidebar rows can also opt into `terminal_title` or `terminal_title_stripped`; neither appears in the default rows. The first shows the latest safety-normalized OSC 0/2 terminal title. The second removes one recognized leading activity or spinner glyph and following whitespace. Herdr owns these values on the server; they are ephemeral across a cold restart and remain independent of metadata titles and semantic agent state.\nL221: Spinner animation can therefore update the raw title without producing a pane update when the stripped text stays the same.\nL222: ## Attach directly to an agent\nL223: \nL224: \ue200cite\ue20235†Section titled “Attach directly to an agent”\ue201 L225: \nL226: Attach your current terminal to one agent terminal instead of the full Herdr UI:\nL227: \nL228: Terminal window\nL229: \nL230:     `\nL231: \nL232:     herdr agent attach reviewer\nL233: \nL234:     `\nL235: \nL236: Detach with `ctrl+b q`. Send a literal `ctrl+b` with `ctrl+b ctrl+b`.\nL237: \nL238: Scroll with the mouse wheel or plain page up/page down. Normal input jumps back to the bottom.\nL239: \nL240: Use `--takeover` if another direct attach client already owns input:\nL241: Terminal window\nL242: \nL243:     `\nL244: \nL245:     herdr agent attach reviewer --takeover\nL246: \nL247:     `\nL248: \nL249: Use `herdr terminal attach <terminal_id>` when you want the same direct attach behavior for a non-agent terminal.\nL250: \nL251: \ue200cite\ue20237†Edit page†github.com\ue201 L252: \nL253: \ue200cite\ue2029†Previous How to work with Herdr\ue201 L254: \ue200cite\ue20211†Next Agent automation\ue201 --------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nherdr/distribution/agent-guide.md at master · herdrdev/herdr · GitHub (https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/master/distribution/agent-guide.md)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn1view3\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: open({"ref_id":"turn0search10","lineno":null}); Total lines: 268\nL196: If you are running inside a Herdr pane (the environment variable `HERDR_ENV=1` is set), Herdr also ships a skill file that teaches you to control Herdr through the `herdr` CLI: \ue200cite\ue20275†https://raw.githubusercontent.com/herdrdev/herdr/master/skills/herdr/SKILL.md†raw.githubusercontent.com\ue201 . That file teaches you to operate Herdr; this one teaches you to guide a human.\nL211: ## Install\nL212: \nL213: Linux and macOS:\nL214: \nL215:     curl -fsSL https://herdr.dev/install.sh | sh\nL216:     herdr\nL217: \nL218: Windows PowerShell:\nL219: \nL220:     powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -c "irm https://herdr.dev/install.ps1 | iex"\nL221:     herdr\nL222: \nL223: If endpoint security blocks that fileless PowerShell command, use Command Prompt:\nL224: \nL225:     curl.exe -fsSLo install.cmd https://herdr.dev/install.cmd && install.cmd && del install.cmd\nL226:     herdr\nL228: ## First-run walkthrough\nL229: \nL230: Check your environment first. If `HERDR_ENV=1` is set, you are already running inside a Herdr pane. The human is already attached, so skip step 1 and never tell them to run `herdr` from your pane; Herdr blocks nested launches by design. Start with step 2, and consider the skill file below.\nL231: \nL232: Walk the human through this sequence:\nL233:   1. `cd` into a project and run `herdr`. It launches or attaches to the default background session and creates a workspace automatically. First run shows an onboarding flow.\nL234:   2. Start their coding agent in the pane — `claude`, `codex`, or any supported agent (full list: \ue200cite\ue20278†https://herdr.dev/docs/agents/†herdr.dev\ue201 ). Herdr detects it automatically; the sidebar shows its state. Install the matching integration when available. Depending on the agent, it provides lifecycle state, native session restore, or both. For example, `herdr integration install claude` adds native session restore, while Claude\'s state still comes from screen detection.\nL235:   3. Start with the mouse: click panes and tabs to focus, drag split borders, right-click for menus, drag-select to copy. No keybindings are required to use Herdr.\nL236:   4. Split panes: right-click menu, or `prefix+v` (right) / `prefix+minus` (down). New tab: `prefix+c`.\nL237:   5. Detach with `prefix+q` (press `ctrl+b`, release, press `q`) or close the terminal window. Everything keeps running. Reattach later with `herdr`.\nL238:   6. To actually stop everything: `herdr server stop`.\nL239: ## The keyboard story\nL240: \nL241: New users do not need to learn keybindings; the mouse covers everything. When the human wants keyboard control:\nL242:   * The prefix key is `ctrl+b` by default. `prefix+?` shows every active binding live.\nL243:   * The guided keyboard page covers the prefix, the bindings to learn first, and a vetted prefix-free setup using `ctrl+alt` chords: \ue200cite\ue20279†https://herdr.dev/docs/keyboard/†herdr.dev\ue201 . Recommend it over improvising.\nL244:   * Every binding, including the prefix itself, is configurable under `[keys]` in the config file.\nL245:   * If a direct chord does nothing, the OS or the outer terminal consumed it before Herdr could see it. The keyboard page explains which chords are safe and why.\nL246: ## Install the Herdr skill into yourself\nL247: \nL248: Herdr ships `skills/herdr/SKILL.md` (\ue200cite\ue20275†https://raw.githubusercontent.com/herdrdev/herdr/master/skills/herdr/SKILL.md†raw.githubusercontent.com\ue201 ), which teaches a coding agent to control Herdr from inside a pane: splitting panes, running commands without stealing focus, reading output, and waiting on other agents.\nL249: Once the human is set up, offer to install it for your coding agent so future sessions can control Herdr directly. For agents supported by the open skills CLI, use `npx skills add herdrdev/herdr --skill herdr -g`. For agents without a skill system, add the GitHub copy above to their global custom instructions. Ask the human before writing to their config locations, and use the GitHub copy above as the source of truth.\nL250: ## Configuration\nL251:   * Config file: `~/.config/herdr/config.toml` on Linux and macOS; `%APPDATA%\\herdr\\config.toml` on Windows. Herdr works without one.\nL252:   * Print the full default config: `herdr --default-config`.\nL253:   * Apply edits to a running server: `herdr server reload-config` (or the global menu → reload config).\nL254:   * Main areas: `[keys]` keybindings, `[theme]` themes, `[ui]` sidebar and UI behavior, `[terminal]` shell defaults, `[update]` channel.\nL255:   * Full reference: \ue200cite\ue20280†https://herdr.dev/docs/configuration/†herdr.dev\ue201 L256: ## Diagnosis recipes\nL257:   * Agent not detected or wrong state: Run `herdr agent list` to see what Herdr sees and `herdr agent explain <target> --json` to see why the detector classified a pane that way. Integrations can provide lifecycle state, native session restore, or both; check `herdr integration status` and the agent support table before assuming an integration replaces screen detection. Details: \ue200cite\ue20278†https://herdr.dev/docs/agents/†herdr.dev\ue201 and \ue200cite\ue20281†https://herdr.dev/docs/integrations/†herdr.dev\ue201 L258:   * A keybinding does nothing: the outer terminal or desktop environment owns that chord. Point the human to \ue200cite\ue20279†https://herdr.dev/docs/keyboard/†herdr.dev\ue201 to pick a safe one or free the chord in their terminal settings.\nL259:   * Something looks wrong at startup or with the socket API: Default-session logs live in `~/.config/herdr/` on Linux and macOS and `%APPDATA%\\herdr\\` on Windows. Named-session logs live under `sessions/<name>/` inside that directory. `herdr status`, `herdr status server`, and `herdr status client` summarize the runtime.\nL260:   * Remote use: SSH to the machine and run `herdr` there (works like tmux), or attach as a thin local client with `herdr --remote <host>`. Trade-offs: \ue200cite\ue20282†https://herdr.dev/docs/how-to-work/†herdr.dev\ue201 L261:   * What survives a detach, restart, or update: \ue200cite\ue20283†https://herdr.dev/docs/session-state/†herdr.dev\ue201 L262: ## Rules for you\nL263:   * Do not invent keybindings, config keys, or CLI flags. The ones in this file are accurate as of writing; for anything else, read the linked docs page first.\nL264:   * Teach mouse before keyboard for humans new to multiplexers.\nL265:   * Herdr is not tmux: do not give tmux commands, tmux config syntax, or `.tmux.conf` advice for Herdr questions.\nL266:   * For automation, scripting, or controlling Herdr from code, point to the CLI reference (\ue200cite\ue20284†https://herdr.dev/docs/cli-reference/†herdr.dev\ue201 ) and socket API (\ue200cite\ue20285†https://herdr.dev/docs/socket-api/†herdr.dev\ue201 ).\nL267: You can’t perform that action at this time.\n'}]

Option 2: Claude Code on native Windows with Git Bash ...   * After global install via npm, use `claude migrate-installer` to move to local\n\n# Set up Claude Code\n\nCopy page\n\nInstall, authenticate, and start using Claude Code on your development machine.\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nSystem requirements\n\n  * Operating Systems: macOS 10.15+, Ubuntu 20.04+/Debian 10+, or Windows 10+ (with WSL 1, WSL 2, or Git for Windows)\n  * Hardware: 4GB+ RAM\n  * Software: Node.js 18+\n  * Network: Internet connection required for authentication and AI processing\n  * Shell: Works best in Bash, Zsh or Fish\n  * Location: Anthropic supported countries\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nStandard installation\n\nTo install Claude Code, run the following command:\n    \n    `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code\n    `\n\nDo NOT use `sudo npm install -g` as this can lead to permission issues and security risks. If you encounter permission errors, see configure Claude Code for recommended solutions.\n\nSome users may be automatically migrated to an improved installation method. Run `claude doctor` after installation to check your installation type.\n\nAfter the installation process completes, navigate to your project and start Claude Code:\n    \n    `cd your-awesome-project\n    claude\n    `\n\nClaude Code offers the following authentication options:\n\n  1. Anthropic Console: The default option. Connect through the Anthropic Console and complete the OAuth process. Requires active billing at console.anthropic.com.\n  2. Claude App (with Pro or Max plan): Subscribe to Claude’s Pro or Max plan for a unified subscription that includes both Claude Code and the web interface. Get more value at the same price point while managing your account in one place. Log in with your Claude.ai account. During launch, choose the option that matches your subscription type.\n  3. Enterprise platforms: Configure Claude Code to use Amazon Bedrock or Google Vertex AI for enterprise deployments with your existing cloud infrastructure.\n\nClaude Code securely stores your credentials. See Credential Management for details.\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nWindows setup\n\nOption 1: Claude Code within WSL\n\n  * Both WSL 1 and WSL 2 are supported\n\nOption 2: Claude Code on native Windows with Git Bash\n\n  * Requires Git for Windows\n  * For portable Git installations, specify the path to your `bash.exe`:\n    \n    `$env:CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH="C:\\Program Files\\Git\\bin\\bash.exe"\n    `\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nAlternative installation methods\n\nClaude Code offers multiple installation methods to suit different environments.\n\nIf you encounter any issues during installation, consult the troubleshooting guide.\n\nRun `claude doctor` after installation to check your installation type and version.\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nGlobal npm installation\n\nTraditional method shown in the install steps above\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nLocal installation\n\n  * After global install via npm, use `claude migrate-installer` to move to local\n  * Avoids autoupdater npm permission issues\n  * Some users may be automatically migrated to this method\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nNative binary installation (Alpha)\n\n  * Use `claude install` from an existing installation\n  * or `curl -fsSL claude.ai/install.sh | bash` for a fresh install\n  * Currently in alpha testing\n  * Platform support: macOS, Linux, Windows (via WSL)\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nRunning on AWS or GCP\n\nBy default, Claude Code uses Anthropic’s API.\n\nFor details on running Claude Code on AWS or GCP, see third-party integrations.\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nUpdate Claude Code\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nAuto updates\n\nClaude Code automatically keeps itself up to date to ensure you have the latest features and security fixes.\n\n  * Update checks: Performed on startup and periodically while running\n  * Update process: Downloads and installs automatically in the background\n  * Notifications: You’ll see a notification when updates are installed\n  * Applying updates: Updates take effect the next time you start Claude Code\n\nDisable auto-updates:\n    \n    `\n    claude config set autoUpdates false --global\n    \n    \n    export DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1\n    `\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nUpdate manually\n    \n    `claude update\n    `--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nCLI reference - Anthropic (https://docs.anthropic.com/en/docs/claude-code/cli-usage)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn2search1\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 1.2 years ago; Complete reference for Claude Code command-line interface, including commands and flags. ... Customize Claude Code’s behavior with these command-line flags:\n\n# CLI reference\n\nCopy page\n\nComplete reference for Claude Code command-line interface, including commands and flags.\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nCLI commands\n\nCommand  | Description  | Example\n--- | --- | ---\n`claude`  | Start interactive REPL  | `claude`\n`claude "query"`  | Start REPL with initial prompt  | `claude "explain this project"`\n`claude -p "query"`  | Query via SDK, then exit  | `claude -p "explain this function"`\n`cat file | claude -p "query"`  | Process piped content  | `cat logs.txt | claude -p "explain"`\n`claude -c`  | Continue most recent conversation  | `claude -c`\n`claude -c -p "query"`  | Continue via SDK  | `claude -c -p "Check for type errors"`\n`claude -r "<session-id>" "query"`  | Resume session by ID  | `claude -r "abc123" "Finish this PR"`\n`claude update`  | Update to latest version  | `claude update`\n`claude mcp`  | Configure Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers  | See the Claude Code MCP documentation.\n\nCLI flags\n\nCustomize Claude Code’s behavior with these command-line flags:\n\nFlag  | Description  | Example\n--- | --- | ---\n`--add-dir`  | Add additional working directories for Claude to access (validates each path exists as a directory)  | `claude --add-dir ../apps ../lib`\n`--allowedTools`  | A list of tools that should be allowed without prompting the user for permission, in addition to settings.json files  | `"Bash(git log:*)" "Bash(git diff:*)" "Read"`\n`--disallowedTools`  | A list of tools that should be disallowed without prompting the user for permission, in addition to settings.json files  | `"Bash(git log:*)" "Bash(git diff:*)" "Edit"`\n`--print`, `-p`  | Print response without interactive mode (see SDK documentation for programmatic usage details)  | `claude -p "query"`\n`--output-format`  | Specify output format for print mode (options: `text`, `json`, `stream-json`)  | `claude -p "query" --output-format json`\n`--input-format`  | Specify input format for print mode (options: `text`, `stream-json`)  | `claude -p --output-format json --input-format stream-json`\n`--verbose`  | Enable verbose logging, shows full turn-by-turn output (helpful for debugging in both print and interactive modes)  | `claude --verbose`\n`--max-turns`  | Limit the number of agentic turns in non-interactive mode  | `claude -p --max-turns 3 "query"`\n`--model`  | Sets the model for the current session with an alias for the latest model (`sonnet` or `opus`) or a model’s full name  | `claude --model claude-sonnet-4-20250514`\n`--permission-mode`  | Begin in a specified permission mode  | `claude --permission-mode plan`\n`--permission-prompt-tool`  | Specify an MCP tool to handle permission prompts in non-interactive mode  | `claude -p --permission-prompt-tool mcp_auth_tool "query"`\n`--resume`  | Resume a specific session by ID, or by choosing in interactive mode  | `claude --resume abc123 "query"`\n`--continue`  | Load the most recent conversation in the current directory  | `claude --continue`\n`--dangerously-skip-permissions`  | Skip permission prompts (use with caution)  | `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions`\n\nThe `--output-format json` flag is particularly useful for scripting and automation, allowing you to parse Claude’s responses programmatically.\n\nFor detailed information about print mode (`-p`) including output formats, streaming, verbose logging, and programmatic usage, see the SDK documentation.\n\n\nSee also\n\n  * Interactive mode - Shortcuts, input modes, and interactive features\n  * Slash commands - Interactive session commands\n  * Quickstart guide - Getting started with Claude Code\n  * Common workflows - Advanced workflows and patterns\n  * Settings - Configuration options\n  * SDK documentation - Programmatic usage and integrations--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nНачало работы с Claude Code - Anthropic (https://docs.anthropic.com/ru/docs/claude-code/getting-started)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn2search2\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 1.3 years ago; В настоящее время Claude Code не работает напрямую в Windows и требует WSL. ...      * Установите с помощью `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code --force --no-os-check` (НЕ используйте `sudo`)\n\nКод Claude\n\nНачало работы с Claude Code\n\nКод Claude\n\n# Начало работы с Claude Code\n\nCopy page\n\nУзнайте, как установить, аутентифицироваться и начать использовать Claude Code.\n\n\nПроверьте системные требования\n\n  * Операционные системы: macOS 10.15+, Ubuntu 20.04+/Debian 10+ или Windows через WSL\n  * Аппаратное обеспечение: минимум 4 ГБ оперативной памяти\n  * Программное обеспечение:\n    * Node.js 18+\n    * git 2.23+ (опционально)\n    * GitHub или GitLab CLI для работы с PR (опционально)\n    * ripgrep (rg) для расширенного поиска файлов (опционально)\n  * Сеть: Требуется подключение к интернету для аутентификации и обработки ИИ\n  * Местоположение: Доступно только в поддерживаемых странах\n\nУстранение проблем с установкой WSL\n\nВ настоящее время Claude Code не работает напрямую в Windows и требует WSL. Если у вас возникли проблемы в WSL:\n\n  1. Проблемы с определением ОС/платформы: Если вы получаете ошибку во время установки, WSL может использовать Windows `npm`. Попробуйте:\n\n     * Выполните `npm config set os linux` перед установкой\n     * Установите с помощью `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code --force --no-os-check` (НЕ используйте `sudo`)\n  2. Ошибки “Node не найден”: Если вы видите `exec: node: not found` при запуске `claude`, ваша среда WSL может использовать Windows-установку Node.js. Вы можете проверить это с помощью `which npm` и `which node`, которые должны указывать на пути Linux, начинающиеся с `/usr/`, а не с `/mnt/c/`. Чтобы исправить это, попробуйте установить Node через менеджер пакетов вашего дистрибутива Linux или через `nvm`.\n\n\nУстановка и аутентификация\n\n1\n\nУстановите Claude Code\n\nInstall NodeJS 18+, then run:\n    \n    `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code\n    `\n\nDo NOT use `sudo npm install -g` as this can lead to permission issues and security risks. This allows you to route all Claude Code traffic through your organization’s proxy servers for security, compliance, and monitoring purposes.\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nBasic proxy configuration\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nEnvironment variables\n\nClaude Code respects standard proxy environment variables:\n    \n    `\n    export HTTPS_PROXY=https://proxy.example.com:8080\n    \n    \n    export HTTP_PROXY=http://proxy.example.com:8080\n    `\n\nClaude Code currently does not support the `NO_PROXY` environment variable. All traffic will be routed through the configured proxy.\n\nClaude Code does not support SOCKS proxies.\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nAuthentication\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nBasic authentication\n\nIf your proxy requires basic authentication, include credentials in the proxy URL:\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nUtilisation des données - Anthropic (https://docs.anthropic.com/fr/docs/claude-code/data-usage)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn2search6\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 1.2 years ago; Claude Code est construit sur les API d’Anthropic.\nCe comportement est configurable.\n\n### \n\n\u200b\n\nMesures de protection de la vie privée\n\nNous avons mis en place plusieurs mesures de protection pour protéger vos données, notamment :\n\n  * Périodes de rétention limitées pour les informations sensibles\n  * Accès restreint aux données de session des utilisateurs\n  * Politiques claires contre l’utilisation des commentaires pour l’entraînement de modèles\n\nPour tous les détails, veuillez consulter nos Conditions générales commerciales et notre Politique de confidentialité.\n\n## \n\n\u200b\n\nFlux de données et dépendances\n\nClaude Code est installé depuis NPM. Claude Code s’exécute localement. Pour interagir avec le LLM, Claude Code envoie des données sur le réseau. Ces données incluent toutes les invites utilisateur et les sorties de modèle. Les données sont chiffrées en transit via TLS et ne sont pas chiffrées au repos. Claude Code est compatible avec la plupart des VPN populaires et des proxies LLM.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nHubungkan Claude Code ke alat melalui MCP - Anthropic (https://docs.anthropic.com/id/docs/claude-code/mcp)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn2search7\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last year;   * Claude Code akan menampilkan peringatan ketika output alat MCP melebihi 10.000 token. ...   * Fitur ini hanya bekerja di macOS dan Windows Subsystem for Linux (WSL)\n\nSetelah dikonfigurasi, Anda dapat mengelola server MCP Anda dengan perintah-perintah ini:\n    \n    `\n    claude mcp list\n    \n    \n    claude mcp get github\n    \n    \n    claude mcp remove github\n    \n    \n    /mcp\n    `\n\nTips:\n\n  * Gunakan flag `--scope` untuk menentukan di mana konfigurasi disimpan:\n    * `local` (default): Hanya tersedia untuk Anda di proyek saat ini (disebut `project` di versi lama)\n    * `project`: Dibagikan dengan semua orang di proyek melalui file `.mcp.json`\n    * `user`: Tersedia untuk Anda di semua proyek (disebut `global` di versi lama)\n  * Atur variabel lingkungan dengan flag `--env` (misalnya, `--env KEY=value`)\n  * Konfigurasi timeout startup server MCP menggunakan variabel lingkungan MCP_TIMEOUT (misalnya, `MCP_TIMEOUT=10000 claude` menetapkan timeout 10 detik)\n  * Claude Code akan menampilkan peringatan ketika output alat MCP melebihi 10.000 token. Untuk meningkatkan batas ini, atur variabel lingkungan `MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (misalnya, `MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS=50000`)\n  * Gunakan `/mcp` untuk mengautentikasi dengan server remote yang memerlukan otentikasi OAuth 2.0\n\nPengguna Windows: Di Windows asli (bukan WSL), server MCP lokal yang menggunakan `npx` memerlukan wrapper `cmd /c` untuk memastikan eksekusi yang tepat.\n    \n    `\n    claude mcp add my-server -- cmd /c npx -y @some/package\n    `\n\nTanpa wrapper `cmd /c`, Anda akan mengalami error “Connection closed” karena Windows tidak dapat langsung mengeksekusi `npx`. Siblings of [[obsidian-command-center]] and [[second-brain-levels]].\r\n\r\n- **"You\'re Using Claude Code Wrong (Add Codex)"** — Chase AI, YouTube `VdxUKiF8CWI`, 2026-05-07. The *why + the tandem workflow*.\r\n- **"How to Use Your Claude Code Projects in Codex in 5 Mins"** — Nate Herk, YouTube `kB9iMD0EjT8`, 2026-05-18. The *how of file structure* — one project, both agents.\r\n\r\n## The pitch: it\'s "and," not "or"\r\n\r\nDon\'t pick Claude Code *or* Codex. Run both on the same project and let each check the other. The Venn diagram is ~99% overlap, so learning one gets you the other for free. The argument underneath is **tool agnosticism**: you owe these companies zero loyalty, models are converging, and pitting them against each other gets better output than staying tribal. Practical upside beyond quality: if one tool is down or stuck, you switch in seconds without losing context.\r\n\r\n## The tandem workflow (Chase)\r\n\r\nRun Codex in its desktop app (openai.com/codex) with a terminal open inside it, then run `claude` in that terminal, so both operate in the same directory. Then bounce work between them:\r\n\r\n- **Plan review.** Codex plans → copy the plan into Claude ("what\'s missing, any blind spots?") → paste Claude\'s critique back → Codex revises. Two expert second-opinions before any code. Works in reverse too.\r\n- **Adversarial code review.** One builds, the other reads the same files and hunts bugs. In the demo Claude found ~20 real bugs on Codex\'s first pass. Telling the reviewer the *other* model wrote it may make it usefully more adversarial. There is a Codex plugin skill for Claude literally called "adversarial review."\r\n- **Cost logic.** Uses more tokens up front, but catching bugs and weak plans early saves tokens in aggregate.\r\n- Desktop-app niceties Chase leans on: in-app browser + sidebar dev server, click-to-annotate/screenshot front-end reviews, diff viewer, "pets" background-activity indicator. Context note: GPT-5.5 Pro ~258k window vs Claude\'s ~1M; new chat = the `/clear` equivalent.\r\n\r\n## The file-structure map (Nate) — one project, both agents\r\n\r\nBoth agents read the **same shared knowledge** (docs, references, scripts, context files). Only the tool-specific wrappers differ:\r\n\r\n| Concern | Claude Code | Codex |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| Instructions file | `CLAUDE.md` | `AGENTS.md` |\r\n| Config folder | `.claude/` | `.codex/` |\r\n| Skills folder | `.claude/skills/` | `.agents/skills/` |\r\n| Skill files | markdown + YAML front matter | **identical** markdown |\r\n| Agent files | markdown | **TOML** (same job, different format) |\r\n| Both support | global (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`) + project-level scope | same |\r\n\r\n**3-layer mental model:** (1) shared knowledge any agent reads unchanged, (2) skills + agents — same content, just placed in each tool\'s folder, (3) tool-specific config that genuinely differs. Maintenance rule: a major change to `CLAUDE.md` gets mirrored to `AGENTS.md` (and skills/agents to their twin). Codex sub-agents don\'t auto-invoke — you call them explicitly.\r\n\r\n**Convert-any-project prompt (Nate\'s):**\r\n\r\n```\r\nI built this project using Claude Code, but I need you, Codex, to be able\r\nto use it too. Create an AGENTS.md that uses the CLAUDE.md as inspiration,\r\ncreate a .codex config, put all skills in .agents, put all agents in .codex\r\n(as TOML). Research both the Codex and Claude Code documentation and make\r\nsure everything important converts over.\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Session handoff** (Nate\'s own skill) is the bridge: summarize decisions / active files / next steps, paste into the other tool, keep going. When one gets stuck, hand off and the other often unsticks it in seconds.\r\n\r\n## Where ai-os stands (gap-check, 2026-07-18)\r\n\r\nai-os **already runs this exact pattern** — it was built dual-runtime from the reform (see `wiki/log.md` 2026-07-17, and [[../decisions/log.md]]). Point-by-point:\r\n\r\n- **`CLAUDE.md` + `AGENTS.md` hand-owned twins** — present, mirrored, near-identical byte size. The only diffs are the *intentional* Claude↔Codex swaps (twin pointer, folder paths, the dual-runtime section written from each runtime\'s view). Voice section is in full parity. **No drift.**\r\n- **`.claude/` and `.codex/` config folders** — both present (`.codex/hooks.json` mirrors the SessionStart bootstrap).\r\n- **Skills as identical markdown, activated by copy** — `.claude/skills/` (22) and `.agents/skills/` (12); Codex runs the same business set minus the wiki/Obsidian/graphify/youtube extras, which is deliberate, not a gap.\r\n- **`session-handoff` skill** — Nate\'s skill, already installed and active in both runtimes.\r\n- **Agents as TOML for Codex** — catalog holds `agents/codex/web-researcher.toml`. Note: that agent is authored to activate **globally** (its module path is `~/.codex/agents/web-search-modules/`), so it is not project-activated into `~/ai-os/.codex/agents/`, and that is by design, not missing wiring.\r\n\r\n**Conclusion:** these clips validate the setup rather than expose work. ai-os conforms to both videos with nothing to fix. The one thing they add that ai-os does *not* lean on is Chase\'s **Codex-desktop-app tandem loop** (in-app browser, annotate-to-comment, the plan↔critique ping-pong) — worth trying when a hard problem stalls Claude, but a workflow habit, not a config change.\r\n\r\n## What\'s new vs already-known\r\n\r\n- **New / worth adopting as a habit:** the plan↔critique and adversarial-review ping-pong between the two models on genuinely hard or high-stakes work; reach for the Codex desktop app\'s live browser + annotation when doing front-end review.\r\n- **Already true in ai-os:** the whole file-structure map, twin instruction files, copy-based skill activation, session-handoff bridge, tool-agnostic stance.\r\n\r\n'}]

the first starts are 1:00 PM, 1:40 PM, 2:20 PM, 3:00 PM, and 3:40 PM America/Toronto.\r\n\r\n## [2026-09-16] system | Immediate consultation cancellation cleanup deployed\r\n\r\n- Diagnosed delayed cancellation cleanup: the cancellation endpoint queued Google Calendar deletion and both cancellation emails but did not wake the existing outbox worker, leaving the scheduler to process them later.\r\n- Pushed LSAT U commit `e0dce3d` to `origin/main`: successful cancellations now immediately wake the worker and invalidate the event's availability cache while preserving the scheduled retry as a fallback.\r\n- Verified six backend contract tests, twelve booking-core tests, TypeScript, the production build, and diff integrity.\r\n- Audited the live database read-only: the prior cancellation waited about 52 seconds for the minute scheduler, while every queued Calendar and email job completed successfully with no error. Both fallback jobs remain active every minute.\r\n- Deployed only the `single-session` Edge Function through Lovable Cloud from commit `e0dce3d`. An independent production `info` request returned HTTP 200 for the active, reusable, free 30-minute consultation event in America/Toronto.\r\n\r\n## 2026-09-16 (conditional diagramming phases)\r\n- Shipped main commit 2290cf0 and verified the new phase instructions on the live General Conditionals activity.\r\n- Questions 1-12 untimed; 13-37 have 45 seconds; 38-62 have 30 seconds. Students diagram with the statement visible; timed choices and retries hide it; feedback restores it.\r\n- 29 focused tests, typecheck, build and responsive browser checks passed.\r\n- First graded assignment timer resume is same-device only; saved repeats sync deadlines across devices.\r\n- Detailed implementation record: brainstorms/2026-09-14-conditional-drill-input-design.md.\r\n\r\n\r\n## 2026-09-17 (paired conditional choices published)\r\n- Published commits 09e37a7, 6d52725, and 486f33b to main and production.\r\n- Four diagram/contrapositive pairs throughout General Conditionals; all62 items checked for exactly one correctpair. Shared refined slash rendering, clearer copy, percentage progress, Exit and progress contrast fixes.\r\n- Preserved saved attempts and scores; fixed obsolete selection handling. Verified Joshua's existing saved session live without submitting answers.\r\n- 39 focused tests, typecheck, production builds and responsive browser verification passed.\r\n\r\n## [2026-09-19] system | Firecrawl reference suite and Gemini workspace routing\r\n- Added canonical Firecrawl integration suite at `references/firecrawl.md` with companion resources in `references/firecrawl/` (`agent-guidelines.md`, `schema-cookbook.md`, `runner.py`).\r\n- Updated `connections.md` Domain 8 (Web access) with verified `/v1/search`, `/v1/scrape`, `/v2/agent` capabilities and `references/firecrawl.md` pointer.\r\n- Synchronized routing map across `GEMINI.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `CLAUDE.md`.\r\n- Created `.gemini/README.md` and routed the dedicated `.gemini/` workspace (`skills/`, `agents/`, `wiki/`, `raw/`, `output/`).\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n## [2026-09-22] ship | LSAT U access fixes live\r\n- Access snapshot of all 41 profiles saved: raw/sources/lsatu-access-snapshots/2026-09-22-profiles-access.sql (restore script).\r\n- PR #130 migration (archive/delete) applied via Lovable (1.7 credits); admin-manage-users deployed; frontend published to lsatprep.study.\r\n- Verified after: 41/41 accounts match the snapshot on all 13 flags, both admin roles intact, no one archived. Prod bundle carries PR #129 realtime and PR #130 archive code.\r\n- Still open: tiers plan (branch docs/student-access-tiers-plan) unapproved; Tier 3 drift question unanswered; 14 classroom-only students left as-is by Joshua's instruction.\r\n"}]

Please reload this page.\nL149: \nL150: ## No results found\nL151: \nL152: \ue200cite\ue20273†View all tags\ue201 L153: \nL154: \ue200cite\ue20274†Image: @github-actions†avatars.githubusercontent.com\ue201 \ue200cite\ue20275†github-actions\ue201 released this 16 Sep 18:40\nL155: \nL156: · \ue200cite\ue20276†51 commits\ue201 to master since this release\nL157: \nL158: Immutable release. Only release title and notes can be modified.\nL159: \nL160: \ue200cite\ue20264†v0.9.1\ue201 L161: \nL162: \ue200cite\ue20277†`065ef9d`\ue201 L163: ### Added\nL164:   * Control agents, panes, workspaces, and worktrees on saved SSH machines with `herdr --machine <label-or-id>`. Commands use the saved machine\'s session without needing an open Herdr window. Update Herdr on both machines to use CLI forwarding; failed remote commands never fall back to Local. (\ue200cite\ue20278†#3918\ue201 )\nL165:   * Connect to Windows SSH hosts from Linux, macOS, or Windows. Interactive setup can install or update the complete Windows package after confirmation; background reconnects never install updates. (\ue200cite\ue20279†#3651\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue20280†#3701\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue20281†#3661\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue20282†#3687\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL166:   * Edit names, filters, and search text at the cursor instead of only at the end. Herdr inputs now support character and word movement, Home/End, deletion, and familiar Ctrl+A/E/K/U/W/Y shortcuts, including Unicode text. (\ue200cite\ue20284†#1803\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue20285†#3698\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20286†@markjaquith\ue201 )\nL167:   * Added Letta Code detection and native conversation restore, including default conversations. Its experimental integration is installed through `herdr integration install letta`, not the Settings integration list or JSON integration API. (\ue200cite\ue20287†#3106\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue20288†#3107\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20289†@just-cameron\ue201 )\nL168:   * Hold Ctrl over a link to highlight it before opening it. Wrapped URLs and OSC 8 links remain clickable even when their beginning or end is outside the viewport. (\ue200cite\ue20290†#1282\ue201 )\nL169:   * Sidebar token rules can now hide matching tokens and their separators with `hide = true`; rows disappear when no tokens remain. (\ue200cite\ue20291†#3925\ue201 )\nL170: ### Changed\nL171:   * Workspace keyboard navigation now spans connected machines in sidebar order, with a visible highlight before Enter switches machines. Collapsed machine and worktree groups remain navigable without accidentally sending actions to the wrong machine. (\ue200cite\ue20292†#3754\ue201 )\nL172:   * Experimental live handoff can now transfer more than 64 panes when the sending server includes this update. An older running server still has its old limit for the first upgrade; this does not change update installation order or make live handoff non-experimental. (\ue200cite\ue20293†#3393\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue20294†#3411\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20295†@kataokatsuki\ue201 )\nL173:   * On Linux and macOS, terminal observers that stop accepting output are disconnected after 30 seconds without write progress. Their pane and other clients keep running; an interrupted stream may end without a final close record. (\ue200cite\ue20296†#3612\ue201 )\nL174:   * Windows terminal cleanup now explicitly resets mouse-reporting modes. The remaining standalone Git Bash detach report in \ue200cite\ue20297†#3748\ue201 is still under investigation; do not treat this release as a confirmed fix for that report. (\ue200cite\ue20298†#4055\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL175: ### Fixed\nL176:   * Remote typing, switching, and popup interaction no longer resend the entire pane screen for small changes. Busy SSH sessions use less bandwidth, and idle attached clients avoid unnecessary redraw work. (\ue200cite\ue20299†#3769\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202100†#3745\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202101†#3822\ue201 )\nL177:   * A stalled SSH machine no longer traps the client away from Local. Clicking a local workspace or agent cancels the unfinished remote switch, and a recovered remote workspace refreshes without an away-and-back selection. Stale screens from before a reconnect are not reused. (\ue200cite\ue202102†#3903\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202103†#3842\ue201 )\nL178:   * Idle SSH connections use less CPU without losing final output. Repeated connection failures back off instead of reconnecting rapidly, and supported idle bridges clean up without stopping remote panes. (\ue200cite\ue202104†#3728\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202105†#4083\ue201 )\nL179:   * Large pastes no longer disconnect the client with an output-queue error. (\ue200cite\ue202106†#3833\ue201 )\nL180:   * SSH setup now shows authentication instructions and useful errors, including Tailscale login URLs and host-key failures, rather than appearing to hang or only reporting a lost connection. `machine add` also accepts options before the SSH target. (\ue200cite\ue202107†#3606\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202108†#3731\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202109†#3883\ue201 )\nL181:   * Machine and worktree groups collapse correctly without changing the selected workspace. The sidebar respects workspace row gaps, stays stable during resizing, and reveals newly focused workspaces. (\ue200cite\ue202110†#3778\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202111†#3738\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202112†#3817\ue201 )\nL182:   * The agent list keeps its scroll position when switching machines. Current-workspace and current-tab filters distinguish identical IDs on different machines. (\ue200cite\ue202113†#3937\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202114†#4101\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202115†#3732\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202116†@minatoaquaMK2\ue201 )\nL183:   * Background machine activation no longer resizes another client\'s focused pane. Window titles follow each client\'s own view rather than another client\'s selection. (\ue200cite\ue202117†#3744\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202118†#4091\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202119†#4130\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL184:   * `agent focus` and `pane move --focus` now move attached clients to the requested pane. Moving without focus no longer leaves a client following a deleted tab or later unrelated focus changes. (\ue200cite\ue202120†#3760\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202121†#4153\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202122†#4171\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202116†@minatoaquaMK2\ue201 )\nL185:   * Creating a worktree focuses its new workspace again. Partially failed worktree removals can be retried, and worktrees with submodules offer an explicit forced-removal choice rather than failing without a recovery action. (\ue200cite\ue202123†#3766\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202124†#3314\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202125†#1797\ue201 )\nL186:   * Temporary worktree-action error banners expire instead of remaining on screen indefinitely. (\ue200cite\ue202126†#1827\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202127†#4063\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL187:   * The last tab remains fully visible when the tab strip overflows, and the session navigator once again draws connected tree branches. (\ue200cite\ue202128†#4151\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202129†#3843\ue201 )\nL188:   * Muted sidebar and tab labels remain readable on dark themes, including Windows Terminal. (\ue200cite\ue202130†#2692\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202131†#4062\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL189:   * Mouse drag and copy-mode selections stay active while output continues, even when part of the selection is outside the viewport. Copying uses the current text in the selected range. Selection repainting also avoids building up a backlog of mouse movements. (\ue200cite\ue202132†#3841\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202133†#3895\ue201 )\nL190:   * Double-click selections remain visible with automatic copy disabled. Holding the second click and dragging extends by whole words, and token selection stops at CJK punctuation. Host selection colors remain visible on transparent backgrounds. (\ue200cite\ue202134†#3847\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202135†#1836\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202136†#3706\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202137†#3798\ue201 )\nL191:   * Scrolling no longer combines mismatched terminal snapshots or leaves stale wrapped rows in fullscreen applications such as Neovim. Alternate-screen history reads preserve content across changing headers and scrollbars. (\ue200cite\ue202138†#3900\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202139†#3329\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202140†#3979\ue201 )\nL192:   * Local Kitty graphics keep their direct transport after the first image. Popups and notifications hide only overlapping image placements; uncovered images remain visible. (\ue200cite\ue202141†#3785\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202142†#4081\ue201 )\nL193:   * Pixel mouse coordinates work independently of image rendering and use the terminal\'s integer cell size, preventing clicks from drifting across the screen. (\ue200cite\ue202143†#3295\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202144†#4140\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL194:   * Function keys F1–F12 are recognized in Kitty keyboard reports, and F1–F4 also work with parameterized reports from terminals such as foot. (\ue200cite\ue202145†#1809\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202146†#2378\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202147†#4067\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202148†@ashokDevs\ue201 and \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL195:   * WezTerm control-key reports now preserve Enter, Backspace, Tab, and Escape. Cmd/Super-modified input reaches pane applications with its modifier intact. Modified Enter stays compatible with panes that have not requested enhanced key encoding. (\ue200cite\ue202149†#3589\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202150†#3710\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202151†#4103\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202152†#4110\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202116†@minatoaquaMK2\ue201 )\nL196:   * Mouse reports split across delayed reads no longer leak their trailing bytes into pane input. Clients reassert mouse reporting after terminal reconnects. (\ue200cite\ue202153†#3911\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202154†#4079\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL197:   * Saved sessions that cannot be loaded are preserved before replacement, rather than silently overwritten. Recovery copies are kept in `session-backups/`; failed preservation leaves the original untouched. (\ue200cite\ue202155†#4125\ue201 )\nL198:   * Deleting a session now requires its exact name, including case, on case-insensitive filesystems. Names beginning with a hyphen can be passed after `--`. (\ue200cite\ue202156†#3819\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202157†#4233\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202158†#3220\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202116†@minatoaquaMK2\ue201 )\nL199:   * Newly started macOS servers retain access to user lookup and DNS across logout. Existing affected servers need a full session restart; live handoff cannot repair their inherited service context. (\ue200cite\ue202159†#4100\ue201 )\nL200:   * Linux process discovery stays responsive around stuck WSL agents and avoids repeatedly scanning an ever-growing process tree. (\ue200cite\ue202160†#2179\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202161†#3621\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202162†#3674\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202163†@dark2momo\ue201 and \ue200cite\ue202164†@caner-akca\ue201 )\nL201:   * Linux desktop notifications identify Herdr as their source. (\ue200cite\ue202165†#3638\ue201 )\nL202:   * `pane split` without a target now splits the calling pane when invoked inside Herdr, rather than another client\'s focused pane. Explicit targets keep their existing behavior. (\ue200cite\ue202166†#4123\ue201 )\nL203:   * Live agent names survive temporary process-detection uncertainty instead of leaving an active agent unreachable by name. (\ue200cite\ue202167†#3225\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202168†#3574\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202164†@caner-akca\ue201 )\nL204:   * Windows Codex prompts reliably submit after pasted text instead of losing Enter, including long prompts. (\ue200cite\ue202169†#3187\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202170†#3961\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL205:   * OpenCode V2 reports lifecycle state from the pane displaying the conversation, so shared servers do not mix up panes. Completed and interrupted turns return to idle, while pending permissions, forms, and failed executions remain blocked. V1 remains supported; restart OpenCode after installing the updated integration. (\ue200cite\ue202171†#3757\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20286†@markjaquith\ue201 )\nL206:   * Codex stays working with static titles, animations disabled, and queued follow-ups. Composer sparkles and old confirmation text no longer make an idle pane appear blocked. (\ue200cite\ue202172†#4092\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202173†#4099\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202174†#3988\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue202116†@minatoaquaMK2\ue201 )\nL207:   * Claude Code\'s Unicode activity spinner and Pi\'s working border are recognized correctly. Cline launcher detection and idle-state reporting are more reliable. (\ue200cite\ue202175†#3949\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202176†#3629\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202177†#2396\ue201 )\nL208:   * Pi records native session paths on Windows. Nested OMP processes no longer replace the parent pane\'s resumable session, and Windows Kimi npm shims are recognized. (\ue200cite\ue202178†#3726\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202179†#2593\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202180†#3994\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202181†#3317\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202182†#4053\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL209:   * Grok updates its restore reference after `/new`, and its imported Claude-compatible hooks no longer overwrite that reference. Reinstall the Claude integration to adopt the narrower hook matching. (\ue200cite\ue202183†#2681\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202184†#4018\ue201 )\nL210:   * Integration configuration updates preserve existing files when writes fail. Hermes installation also preserves valid YAML when enabled plugins use an inline list. (\ue200cite\ue202185†#3970\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202186†#3839\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202187†#4056\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL211:   * Plugin registry writes preserve symlinks. Plugins keep using the running server\'s compatible binary after a client-only update, and manual pane navigation emits focus events again. (\ue200cite\ue202188†#3922\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202189†#3824\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202190†#4077\ue201 )\nL212:   * Config files containing a UTF-8 BOM load and save correctly. The default config places `ui.accent` in the right table. (\ue200cite\ue202191†#3678\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202192†#3962\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202193†#2697\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL213:   * Windows local and SSH input preserves Alt combinations, Ctrl+S, and enhanced keyboard text instead of dropping keys or inserting garbage. Remote dead-key input no longer adds an extra base character. (\ue200cite\ue202194†#3702\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202195†#3910\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202196†#3932\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202197†#3948\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202198†#4133\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202199†#4148\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL214:   * Windows mouse capture preserves SGR coordinates, including past column 95, and reattaching restores mouse reporting. Legacy SSH mouse reports no longer produce phantom characters or block subsequent input. (\ue200cite\ue202200†#4155\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202201†#3735\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202202†#4080\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL215:   * Git Bash SSH connections honor host aliases from the user\'s SSH config. (\ue200cite\ue202203†#3947\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202204†#4054\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL216:   * Windows login-mode panes honor the configured shell and keep PowerShell working-directory reports current. Pane launch paths use consistent directory casing. (\ue200cite\ue202205†#1445\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202206†#4060\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202207†#4065\ue201 , \ue200cite\ue202208†#4199\ue201 , thanks \ue200cite\ue20283†@JJLiebig\ue201 )\nL217: ### Contributors\nL218: \nL219:   *   *   *   *   *   *   *   * markjaquith, just-cameron, and 6 other contributors\nL220: \nL221: Assets 8\nL222: \nL223: Loading\nL224: ### Uh oh!\nL225: \nL226: There was an error while loading. Windows support is experimental and may require WSL.\n\n# OpenAI Codex CLI – Getting Started\n\nUpdated: 3 months ago\n\n# Overview\n\nOpenAI Codex CLI is an open‑source command‑line tool that brings the power of our latest reasoning models directly to your terminal. It acts as a lightweight coding agent that can read, modify, and run code on your local machine to help you build features faster, squash bugs, and understand unfamiliar code. Because the CLI runs locally, your source code never leaves your environment unless you choose to share it.\n\niframe\n\nFor more details about Codex, please check out the Github repo directly - https://github.com/openai/codex.\n\n# Key Functionality\n\n  * Zero‑setup installation – a single `npm install -g @openai/codex` (or `codex --upgrade`) gets you started.\n\n  * Multimodal inputs – pass text, screenshots, or diagrams and let the agent generate or edit code accordingly.\n\n  * Rich approvals workflow – choose how hands‑on you want to be with three distinct modes (see Approval Modes).\n\n  * Runs entirely in your terminal – perfect for quick iteration without switching contexts.\n\n# Approval Modes\n\nMode  | What the Agent Can Do  | When to Use\nSuggest (default)  | Read files. Proposes edits & shell commands, but requires your approval before making changes or executing commands.  | Safe exploration, code reviews, learning a codebase.\nAuto Edit  | Read and write files automatically. Still asks before running shell commands.  | Refactoring or repetitive edits where you want to keep an eye on side‑effects.\nFull Auto  | Read, write, and execute commands autonomously inside a sandboxed, network‑disabled environment scoped to the current directory.  | Longer tasks like fixing a broken build or prototyping features while you grab a coffee.\n\nPro tip: Codex warns you before entering Auto Edit or Full Auto if the directory isn’t under version control.\n\n# Quick Start\n\n  1. Install: `npm install -g @openai/codex`\n\n  2. Authenticate: Export your OpenAI API key (`export OPENAI_API_KEY="<OAI_KEY>"`).\n\n  3. Run in Suggest mode: From your project root, type codex and ask for example: “Explain this repo to me.”\n\n  4. Switch modes: Add flags as needed:\n\n     * `codex --auto-edit`\n\n     * `codex --full-auto`\n\n  5. Review outputs: Codex prints proposed patches and shell commands inline. Approve, reject, or tweak as desired.\n\n# FAQ\n\n## Which models does Codex use?\n\nBy default Codex targets o4‑mini for fast reasoning. You can specify any model available in the Responses API, e.g. `codex -m o3`. We’d also recommend verifying your developer account to start seeing chain of thought summaries in the API.\n\n## Does Codex upload my code to OpenAI?\n\nNo. All file reads, writes, and command executions happen locally. Only your prompt, high‑level context, and optional diff summaries are sent to the model for generation.\n\n## How do I change approval modes?\n\nStart Codex with the appropriate flag (`--suggest`, `--auto-edit`, `--full-auto`) or use the in‑session shortcut `/mode` to toggle.\n\n## What platforms are supported?\n\nCodex officially supports macOS and Linux. Windows support is experimental and may require WSL.\n\n## The agent is stuck or times out—what can I do?\n\n  * Ensure you have a stable internet connection for API calls.\n\n  * Try `CTRL‑C` to cancel the current step and ask Codex to continue.\n\n  * If using Full Auto mode, confirm that the sandbox has permission to access required directories.\n\n## How do I update Codex?\n\nRun `codex --upgrade` at any time to fetch the latest release.\n\n## Where can I report issues or ask questions?\n\nOpen a discussion or file an issue in the GitHub repo or engage with other developers in the community via the OpenAI Developer Community.\n\n## Was this article helpful?\n\n[Input]\n\n[Input]\n\nSubmit--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nSelf-hosted sandboxes | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agents-api/environments/self-hosted)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn4search1\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Create the working directory and install the Codex CLI inside the environment. ...     npm install -g @openai/codex@alpha`\n\nCreate the working directory and install the Codex CLI inside the environment. This example uses `/workspace`:\n    \n    `mkdir -p /workspace\n    npm install -g @openai/codex@alpha`\n\n### Network access\n\nAllow outbound connections to these hosts:\n\n  * `https://api.openai.com` for environment registration.\n  * `wss://codex-cloud-environments.chatgpt.com` for commands and results.\n\n### Authentication\n\nUse `OPENAI_API_KEY` for application requests. Grant it `api.agents.read` and `api.agents.write` for session operations, plus `api.responses.write` for model inference. Add `api.vaults.read` and `api.vaults.write` if your application manages vaults.\n\nCreate a separate environment key on the Agents tab in the platform dashboard. It must belong to the same organization, project, and user or service account that owns the session. Set every other permission to None.\n\nSet `OPENAI_EXECUTOR_API_KEY` to this environment key in your application or provisioning service. Pass its value into the sandbox as `CODEX_API_KEY`, which `codex exec-server` reads. Keep your application’s `OPENAI_API_KEY` outside the sandbox.\n\nAgent-generated code can read the environment key, but the key only permits connecting environments. It cannot authorize any other API action. Keep it out of source code, container images, and logs. Rotate or revoke it when needed.\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nUsing Goals in Codex (https://developers.openai.com/cookbook/examples/codex/using_goals_in_codex)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn4search2\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 4 months ago; Crawled: today; To use Goals, install or update Codex and confirm your version. ...     `npm install -g @openai/codex@latest\n## Quickstart: using Goals\n\nUse a Goal when the task has a clear finish line but the path to that finish line is uncertain. Good candidates include performance optimization, flaky test investigation, dependency migrations, bug hunts that require reproduction, multi-step refactors, benchmark-driven tuning, and research tasks that require a final artifact. A normal prompt remains the right tool for a one-off edit.\n\nTo use Goals, install or update Codex and confirm your version. Goals are available starting in Codex 0.128.0.\n\nWith npm:\n    \n    `npm install -g @openai/codex@latest\n    codex --version`\n\nWith Homebrew:\n    \n    `brew update\n    brew upgrade --cask codex\n    codex --version`\n\nThen set a Goal with /goal followed by the outcome:\n    \n    `/goal Reduce p95 latency below 120 ms without regressing correctness tests`\n\nYou can manage the lifecycle from the same command surface:\n    \n    `/goal      \tView the current Goal\n    /goal pause    Pause an active Goal\n    /goal resume   Resume a paused Goal\n    /goal clear    Remove the current Goal`\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nCodex CLI and Sign in with ChatGPT | OpenAI Help Center (https://help.openai.com/en/articles/11381614-api-codex-cli-and-sign-in-with-chatgpt)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn4search3\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 1.2 years ago; Install the CLI. ... Make sure you’re using the latest version of Codex CLI: `npm i -g @openai/codex`\n\n# Codex CLI and Sign in with ChatGPT\n\nUpdated: 2 months ago\n\nThis article covers Codex CLI and the new Sign in with ChatGPT flow for the Codex CLI that links your ChatGPT identity to an API account in one step.\n\n# Access & incentives\n\n## Who can use the new sign-in flow?\n\n  * Free, Plus, and Pro ChatGPT accounts.\n\n  * Enterprise, Edu, and Team workspaces are excluded for now.\n\n## How long is the launch-promo credit offer available?\n\n  * Complete the sign-in flow by June 15 to receive promo credits.\n\n## What is the `codex-mini-latest` model?\n\n  * `codex-mini-latest` is an o4-mini snapshot tuned for Codex CLI workflows. See more details on our platform docs.\n\n  * Price: $1.50 / M input tokens, $6 / M output tokens.\n\n  * `codex-mini-latest` is not recommended for general API usage. The snapshot will refresh regularly as we improve the underlying model for Codex CLI use cases.\n\n## What credits do I receive?\n\nPlus users who Sign in with ChatGPT to the Codex CLI receive $5 in API credits, and Pro users receive $50 in API credits. All promotional credits expire 30 days after they’re granted.\n\nTo be eligible to receive Sign in with ChatGPT promotional credits, ChatGPT Plus or Pro subscriptions must be >7 days old & have a default payment method set in the selected API org.\n\n# Setup\n\n## How do I connect my ChatGPT account to Codex CLI?\n\n  1. Install the CLI.\n\n  2. Run the following command.\n    \n    `codex --login`\n\n  3. Click Sign in with ChatGPT.\n\n     * The flow links your ChatGPT identity to an API account and stores credentials locally.\n\n  4. Start prompting in your terminal.\n\n## Do I get API keys?\n\nThe flow creates a key for you automatically; no manual key copy-paste required.\n\n# Security & data sharing\n\n## Is data shared with OpenAI for training?\n\nBy default, OpenAI does not use any inputs or outputs from our products for business users, including ChatGPT Team, ChatGPT Enterprise, and the API, to improve our models. However, API organization owners can choose to opt-in to share API data with OpenAI. This setting is not available to certain organizations, including Enterprise and customers with Zero Data Retention enabled.\nAny API keys it created remain active until you revoke them from the API dashboard.”\n\n  * Key‑revoke modal: “This API key will immediately be disabled. API requests made using this key will be rejected… Once revoked, you’ll no longer be able to view or modify this API key.”\n\nImage\n\n## Scenario\u202f4:\n\n### Are ChatGPT and API logins still tied together, and what’s the rule now?\n\nYes. You sign in once with your OpenAI identity, but the ChatGPT session token and CLI‑generated secret keys are separate. Revoking one does not affect the other; you must disconnect the OAuth grant and, if desired, delete the key.\n\n# Limitations\n\n  1. You can use `codex-mini-latest` outside the Codex CLI through the Responses API, but we recommend using gpt-4.1 for most tasks. The `codex-mini-latest` model is optimized for use in Codex CLI.\n\n  2. Network disabled during execution: integration tests that call external services will fail.\n\n  3. Enterprise sign-in support: coming later; use traditional API key provisioning for now.\n\n# FAQ\n\n## Having Trouble Redeeming Credit?\n\nWe’ve made it easier to redeem credits (if you’re eligible).
